"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[e](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") **Natural language processing** (**NLP**) is the processing of [natural language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") information by a [computer](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"). NLP is a subfield of [computer science](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and is closely associated with [artificial intelligence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"). NLP is also related to [information retrieval](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), [knowledge representation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), [computational linguistics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), and [linguistics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") more
broadly.[[1]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")

Major processing tasks in an NLP system include: [speech recognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), [text classification](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), [natural language understanding](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), and [natural language generation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles").


## History
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]

Further information: [History of natural language processing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")
Natural language processing has its roots in the 1950s.[[2]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")
Already in 1950, [Alan Turing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") published an article titled "[Computing Machinery and Intelligence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")," which proposed what is now called the [Turing test](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") as a criterion of intelligence, though at the time that was not articulated as a problem separate from artificial intelligence. The proposed test includes a task that involves the automated interpretation and generation of natural language.


### Symbolic NLP (1950s – early 1990s)
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")][![application/x-wiki](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")
A document parsed into an abstract syntax tree
The premise of symbolic NLP is often illustrated using [John Searle's Chinese room](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") thought experiment: Given a collection of rules (e.g., a Chinese phrasebook, with questions and matching answers), the computer emulates natural language understanding (or other NLP tasks) by applying those rules to the data it confronts.

- **1950s**: The [Georgetown experiment](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") in 1954 involved fully [automatic translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") of more than sixty Russian sentences into English. The authors claimed that within three or five years, machine translation would be a solved problem.[[3]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")  However, real progress was much slower, and after the [ALPAC report](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") in 1966, which found that ten years of research had failed to fulfill the expectations, funding for machine translation was dramatically reduced. Little further research in machine translation was conducted in America (though some research continued elsewhere, such as Japan and Europe[[4]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) until the late 1980s when the first [statistical machine translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") systems were developed.
- **1960s**: Some notably successful natural language processing systems developed in the 1960s were [SHRDLU](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), a natural language system working in restricted "[blocks worlds](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")" with restricted vocabularies, and [ELIZA](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), a simulation of Rogerian psychotherapy, written by [Joseph Weizenbaum](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") between 1964 and 1966. Despite using minimal information about human thought or emotion, ELIZA was able to produce interactions that appeared human-like. When the "patient" exceeded the very small knowledge base, ELIZA might provide a generic response, for example, responding to "My head hurts" with "Why do you say your head hurts?". Ross Quillian's successful work on natural language was demonstrated with a vocabulary of only *twenty* words, because that was all that would fit in a computer memory at the time.[[5]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")
- **1970s**: During the 1970s, many programmers began to write "conceptual [ontologies](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")", which structured real-world information into computer-understandable data.  Examples are MARGIE (Schank, 1975), SAM (Cullingford, 1978), PAM (Wilensky, 1978), TaleSpin (Meehan, 1976), QUALM (Lehnert, 1977), Politics (Carbonell, 1979), and Plot Units (Lehnert 1981). During this time, the first [chatterbots](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") were written (e.g., [PARRY](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")).
- **1980s**: The 1980s and early 1990s mark the heyday of symbolic methods in NLP. Focus areas of the time included research on rule-based parsing (e.g., the development of [HPSG](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") as a computational operationalization of [generative grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")), morphology (e.g., two-level morphology[[6]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")), semantics (e.g., [Lesk algorithm](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")), reference (e.g., within Centering Theory[[7]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) and other areas of natural language understanding (e.g., in the [Rhetorical Structure Theory](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")). Other lines of research were continued, e.g., the development of chatterbots with [Racter](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") and [Jabberwacky](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"). An important development (that eventually led to the statistical turn in the 1990s) was the rising importance of quantitative evaluation in this period.[[8]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")


### Statistical NLP (1990s–present)
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")]
Up until the 1980s, most natural language processing systems were based on complex
sets of hand-written rules. Starting in the late 1980s, however, there was a revolution
in natural language processing with the introduction of [machine learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") algorithms for language processing. This shift was influenced by increasing computational power (see [Moore's law](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) and a decline in the dominance of [Chomskyan](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") linguistic theories (e.g. [transformational grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")), whose theoretical underpinnings discouraged the sort of [corpus linguistics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") that underlies the machine-learning approach to language
processing.[[9]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")

- **1990s:** Many early successes of statistical NLP occurred in [machine translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), particularly through work at [IBM Research](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"). An IBM team including [Frederick Jelinek](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), [Peter F. Brown](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), and [Robert Mercer](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") developed a probabilistic approach to translation, described in the 1990 paper *A Statistical Approach to Machine Translation*.[[10]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") The work used parallel English and French text from the proceedings of the [Parliament of Canada](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") and was subsequently developed into the [IBM alignment models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles").[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")[[12]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") However, such systems often depended on large task-specific corpora, making the availability of suitable training data an important limitation.
- **2000s**: With the growth of the web, increasing amounts of raw (unannotated) language data have become available since the mid-1990s. Research has thus increasingly focused on [unsupervised](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and [semi-supervised learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") algorithms. Such algorithms can learn from data that has not been hand-annotated with the desired answers or using a combination of annotated and non-annotated data. Generally, this task is much more difficult than [supervised learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), and typically produces less accurate results for a given amount of input data. However, large quantities of non-annotated data are available (including, among other things, the entire content of the [World Wide Web](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")), which can often make up for the worse efficiency if the algorithm used has a low enough [time complexity](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") to be practical.
- **2003:** [word n-gram model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), at the time the best statistical algorithm, is outperformed by a [multi-layer perceptron](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (with a single hidden layer and context length of several words, trained on up to 14 million words, by [Bengio](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") et al.)[[13]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
- **2010:** [Tomáš Mikolov](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (then a PhD student at [Brno University of Technology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")) with co-authors applied a simple [recurrent neural network](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") with a single hidden layer to language modeling,[[14]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and in the following years he went on to develop [Word2vec](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"). In the 2010s, [representation learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and [deep neural network](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")-style (featuring many hidden layers) machine learning methods became widespread in natural language processing. This shift gained momentum due to results showing that such techniques[[15]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[16]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") can achieve state-of-the-art results in many natural language tasks, e.g., in [language modeling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[17]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and parsing.[[18]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[19]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") This is increasingly important [in medicine and healthcare](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), where NLP helps analyze notes and text in [electronic health records](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") that would otherwise be inaccessible for study when seeking to improve care[[20]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") or protect patient privacy.[[21]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")


## Approaches: Symbolic, statistical, neural networks
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]
Symbolic approach, i.e., the hand-coding of a set of rules for manipulating symbols,
coupled with a dictionary lookup, was historically the first approach used both by
AI in general and by NLP in particular:[[22]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[23]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
such as by writing grammars or devising heuristic rules for [stemming](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles").

[Machine learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") approaches, which include both statistical and neural networks, on the other hand, have many advantages over the symbolic approach:

- both statistical and neural network methods tend to focus more on the most common cases extracted from a corpus of texts, whereas the rule-based approach needs to provide rules for both rare and common cases equally.
- [language models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), produced by either statistical or neural network methods, are more robust to both unfamiliar (e.g. containing words or structures that have not been seen before) and erroneous input (e.g. with misspelled words or words accidentally omitted) in comparison to the rule-based systems, which are also more costly to produce.
- the larger such a (probabilistic) language model is, the more accurate it becomes, in contrast to rule-based systems that can gain accuracy only by increasing the amount and complexity of the rules leading to [intractability](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") problems.

Rule-based systems are commonly used:

- when the amount of training data is insufficient to successfully apply machine learning methods, e.g., for the machine translation of low-resource languages such as provided by the [Apertium](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") system,
- for preprocessing in NLP pipelines, e.g., [tokenization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), or
- for post-processing and transforming the output of NLP pipelines, e.g., for [knowledge extraction](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") from syntactic parses.


### Statistical approach
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]
In the late 1980s and mid-1990s, the statistical approach ended a period of [AI winter](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), which was caused by the inefficiencies of the rule-based
approaches.[[24]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[25]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")

The earliest [decision trees](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), producing systems of hard [if–then rules](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), were still very similar to the old rule-based approaches.Only the introduction of hidden [Markov models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), applied to part-of-speech tagging, announced the end of the old rule-based approach.


### Neural networks
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]

Further information: [Artificial neural network](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
A major drawback of statistical methods is that they require elaborate [feature engineering](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"). Since
2015,[[26]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
[neural network](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")–based methods have increasingly replaced traditional statistical approaches, using [semantic networks](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[27]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
and [word embeddings](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") to capture semantic properties of words.

Intermediate tasks (e.g., part-of-speech tagging and dependency parsing) are not
needed anymore.

[Neural machine translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), based on the then-newly invented [sequence-to-sequence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") transformations, made obsolete the intermediate steps, such as word alignment, previously necessary for [statistical machine translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles").


## Common NLP tasks
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]
The following is a list of some of the most commonly researched tasks in natural
language processing. Some of these tasks have direct real-world applications, while
others more commonly serve as subtasks that are used to aid in solving larger tasks.

Though natural language processing tasks are closely intertwined, they can be subdivided
into categories for convenience. A coarse division is given below.


### Text and speech processing
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")][![application/x-wiki](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Word cloud of stop words in Hebrew[Optical character recognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(OCR)Given an image representing printed text, determine the corresponding text.[Speech recognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a sound clip of a person or people speaking,
determine the textual representation of the speech.  This is the opposite of [text to speech](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and is one of the extremely difficult problems colloquially termed "[AI-complete](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")" (see above).  In [natural speech](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") there are hardly any pauses between successive words, and thus [speech segmentation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") is a necessary subtask of speech recognition (see below). In most spoken languages, the sounds representing successive letters blend into each other in a process termed [coarticulation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), so the conversion of the [analog signal](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") to discrete characters can be a very difficult process. Also, given that words in the same language are spoken by people with different accents, the speech recognition software must be able to recognize the wide variety of input as being identical to each other in terms of its textual equivalent.[Speech segmentation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a sound clip of a person or people speaking,
separate it into words.  A subtask of [speech recognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and typically grouped with it.[Text-to-speech](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a text, transform those units and produce a
spoken representation. Text-to-speech can be used to aid the visually impaired.[[28]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Word segmentation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") ([Tokenization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"))[Tokenization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") is a text-processing technique that divides text into individual words or word fragments. This technique results in two key components: a word index and tokenized text. The word index is a list that maps unique words to specific numerical identifiers, and the tokenized text replaces each word with its corresponding numerical token. These numerical tokens are then used in various deep learning
methods.[[29]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")For a language like [English](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), this is fairly trivial, since words are usually separated by spaces. However, some written languages like [Chinese](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), [Japanese](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and [Thai](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") do not mark word boundaries in such a fashion, and in those languages text segmentation is a significant task requiring knowledge of the [vocabulary](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and [morphology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") of words in the language. Sometimes this process is also used in cases like [bag of words](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (BOW) creation in data
mining.[[30]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[31]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")


### Morphological analysis
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")][![application/x-wiki](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Lemmatization of Basque words[Lemmatization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")The task of removing inflectional endings only and
returning the base dictionary form of a word which is also known as a lemma. Lemmatization
is another technique for reducing words to their normalized form. But in this case,
the transformation actually uses a dictionary to map words to their actual form.[[32]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Morphological segmentation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Separate words into individual [morphemes](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and identify the class of the morphemes. The difficulty of this task depends greatly on the complexity of the [morphology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(*i.e.*, the structure of words) of the language being considered. [English](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") has fairly simple morphology, especially [inflectional morphology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), and thus it is often possible to ignore this task entirely and simply model all possible forms of a word (e.g., "open, opens, opened, opening") as separate words. In languages such as [Turkish](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") or [Meitei](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), a highly [agglutinated](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") Indian language, however, such an approach is not possible, as each dictionary entry has thousands of possible word
forms.[[33]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Part-of-speech tagging](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a sentence, determine the [part of speech](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (POS) for each word. Many words, especially common ones, can serve as multiple parts of speech. For example, "book" can be a [noun](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") ("the book on the table") or [verb](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") ("to book a flight"); "set" can be a noun, verb or [adjective](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"); and "out" can be any of at least five different parts of speech.[Stemming](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")The process of reducing inflected (or sometimes derived)
words to a base form (e.g., "close" will be the root for "closed", "closing", "close",
"closer" etc.). Stemming yields similar results as lemmatization, but does so on
grounds of rules, not a dictionary.


### Syntactic analysis

[Grammar induction](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[34]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Generate a [formal grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") that describes a language's syntax.[Sentence breaking](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (also known as "[sentence boundary disambiguation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")")Given a chunk of text, find the sentence boundaries.
Sentence boundaries are often marked by [periods](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") or other [punctuation marks](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), but these same characters can serve other purposes (e.g., marking [abbreviations](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")).[Parsing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Determine the [parse tree](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (grammatical analysis) of a given sentence. The [grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") for [natural languages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") is [ambiguous](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") and typical sentences have multiple possible analyses: perhaps surprisingly, for a typical sentence there may be thousands of potential parses (most of which will seem completely nonsensical to a human). There are two primary types of parsing:
*dependency parsing* and *constituency parsing*. Dependency parsing focuses on the
relationships between words in a sentence (marking things like primary objects and
predicates), whereas constituency parsing focuses on building out the parse tree
using a [probabilistic context-free grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (PCFG) (see also *[stochastic grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")*).


### Lexical semantics (of individual words in context)
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")][![application/x-wiki](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
An entity linking pipeline[Lexical semantics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")What is the computational meaning of individual words
in context?[Distributional semantics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")How can we learn semantic representations from data?[Named entity recognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(NER)Given a stream of text, determine which items in the text map to proper names,
such as people or places, and what the type of each such name is (e.g. person, location,
organization). Although [capitalization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") can aid in recognizing named entities in languages such as English, this information cannot aid in determining the type of [named entity](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), and in any case, is often inaccurate or insufficient.  For example, the first letter of a sentence is also capitalized, and named entities often span several words, only some of which are capitalized.  Furthermore, many other languages in non-Western scripts (e.g. [Chinese](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") or [Arabic](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) do not have any capitalization at all, and even languages with capitalization may not consistently use it to distinguish names. For example, [German](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") capitalizes all [nouns](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), regardless of whether they are names, and [French](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") and [Spanish](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") do not capitalize names that serve as [adjectives](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"). This task is also referred to as token
classification.[[35]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")[Sentiment analysis](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") (see also [Multimodal sentiment analysis](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia"))Sentiment analysis involves identifying and classifying the emotional
tone expressed in text. This technique involves analyzing text to determine whether
the expressed sentiment is positive, negative, or neutral. Models for sentiment classification
typically utilize inputs such as [word n-grams](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"), [Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") (TF-IDF) features, hand-generated features, or employ [deep learning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") models designed to recognize both long-term and short-term dependencies in text sequences. The applications of sentiment analysis are diverse, extending to tasks such as categorizing customer reviews on various online
platforms.[[29]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")[Terminology extraction](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")The goal of terminology extraction is to automatically extract relevant
terms from a given corpus.[Word-sense disambiguation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")
(WSD)Many words have more than one [meaning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"); we have to select the meaning which makes the most sense in context.  For this problem, we are typically given a list of words and associated word senses, e.g. from a dictionary or an online resource such as [WordNet](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia").[Entity linking](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")Many words—typically proper names—refer to [named entities](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"); here we have to select the entity (a famous individual, a location, a company, etc.) which is referred to in context.


### Relational semantics (semantics of individual sentences)
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")][Relationship extraction](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")Given a chunk of text, identify the relationships among named entities
(e.g. who is married to whom).[Semantic parsing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")Given a piece of text (typically a sentence), produce a formal representation
of its semantics, either as a graph (e.g., in [AMR parsing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) or in accordance with a logical formalism (e.g., in [DRT parsing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")). This challenge typically includes aspects of several more elementary NLP tasks from semantics (e.g., semantic role labelling, word-sense disambiguation) and can be extended to include full-fledged discourse analysis (e.g., discourse analysis, coreference; see [Natural language understanding](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") below).[Semantic role labelling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") (see also implicit semantic role labelling
below)Given a single sentence, identify and disambiguate semantic predicates (e.g.,
verbal [frames](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")), then identify and classify the frame elements ([semantic roles](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")).


### Discourse (semantics beyond individual sentences)
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")][Coreference resolution](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")Given a sentence or larger chunk of text, determine which words ("mentions")
refer to the same objects ("entities"). [Anaphora resolution](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") is a specific example of this task, and is specifically concerned with matching up [pronouns](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") with the nouns or names to which they refer. The more general task of coreference resolution also includes identifying so-called "bridging relationships" involving [referring expressions](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia"). For example, in a sentence such as "He entered John's house through the front door", "the front door" is a referring expression and the bridging relationship to be identified is the fact that the door being referred to is the front door of John's house (rather than of some other structure that might also be referred to).[Discourse analysis](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")This rubric includes several related tasks. One task is discourse parsing,
i.e., identifying the [discourse](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") structure of a connected text, i.e. the nature of the discourse relationships between sentences (e.g. elaboration, explanation, contrast). Another possible task is recognizing and classifying the [speech acts](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") in a chunk of text (e.g. yes–no question, content question, statement, assertion,
etc.).Implicit semantic role labellingGiven a single sentence, identify and disambiguate
semantic predicates (e.g., verbal [frames](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")) and their explicit semantic roles in the current sentence (see [Semantic role labelling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") above). Then, identify semantic roles that are not explicitly realized in the current sentence, classify them into arguments that are explicitly realized elsewhere in the text and those that are not specified, and resolve the former against the local text. A closely related task is zero anaphora resolution, i.e., the extension of coreference resolution to [pro-drop languages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia").[Recognizing textual entailment](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")Given two text fragments, determine if one being true entails the other,
entails the other's negation, or allows the other to be either true or false.[[36]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia")[Topic segmentation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") and
recognitionGiven a chunk of text, separate it into segments each of which is devoted
to a topic, and identify the topic of the segment.[Argument mining](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing -
Wikipedia")The goal of argument mining is the automatic extraction and identification
of argumentative structures from [natural language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Natural language processing - Wikipedia") text with the aid of computer
programs.[[37]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard")
Such argumentative structures include the premise, conclusions, the [argument scheme](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") and the relationship between the main and subsidiary argument, or the main and counter-argument within
discourse.[[38]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")[[39]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")


### Higher-level NLP applications
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")][![application/x-wiki](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=startup&only=scripts&raw=1&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Machine translation in Firefox[Automatic summarization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (text
summarization)Produce a readable summary of a chunk of text.  Often used to provide
summaries of the text of a known type, such as research papers, articles in the financial
section of a newspaper.Grammatical error correctionGrammatical error detection and
correction involves a great bandwidth of problems on all levels of linguistic analysis
(phonology/orthography, morphology, syntax, semantics, pragmatics). Grammatical error
correction is impactful since it affects hundreds of millions of people who use or
acquire English as a second language. It has thus been subject to a number of shared
tasks since 2011.[[40]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[41]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[42]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
As far as orthography, morphology, syntax and certain aspects of semantics are concerned,
and due to the development of powerful neural language models such as [GPT-2](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.math.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), this can now (2019) be considered a largely solved problem and is being marketed in various commercial applications.[Logic translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Translate a text from a natural language into formal
logic.[Machine translation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(MT)Automatically translate text from one human language to another.  This is one
of the most difficult problems, and is a member of a class of problems colloquially
termed "[AI-complete](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")", i.e. requiring all of the different types of knowledge that humans possess (grammar, semantics, facts about the real world, etc.) to solve properly.[Natural language understanding](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(NLU)Convert chunks of text into more formal representations such as [first-order logic](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") structures that are easier for [computer](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") programs to manipulate. Natural language understanding involves the identification of the intended semantic from the multiple possible semantics that can be derived from a natural language expression which usually takes the form of organized notations of natural language concepts. Introduction and creation of language metamodels and ontologies are efficient but empirical solutions. An explicit formalization of natural language semantics without confusion with implicit assumptions such as [closed-world assumption](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (CWA) vs. [open-world assumption](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), or subjective Yes/No vs. objective True/False is expected for the construction of a basis of semantics
formalization.[[43]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Natural language generation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
(NLG):Convert information from computer databases or semantic intents into readable
human language.Book generationNot an NLP task proper but an extension of natural
language generation and other NLP tasks is the creation of full-fledged books. The
first machine-generated book was created by a rule-based system in 1984 (Racter,
*The policeman's beard is half-constructed*).[[44]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
The first published work by a neural network was published in 2018, *[1 the Road](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")*, marketed as a novel, contains sixty million words.
Both these systems are basically elaborate but nonsensical (semantics-free) [language models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"). The first machine-generated science book was published in 2019 (Beta Writer,
*Lithium-Ion Batteries*, Springer, Cham).[[45]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Unlike *Racter* and *1 the Road*, this is grounded on factual knowledge and based
on text summarization.[Document AI](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")A Document AI platform sits on top of the NLP technology
enabling users with no prior experience of artificial intelligence, machine learning
or NLP to quickly train a computer to extract the specific data they need from different
document types. NLP-powered Document AI enables non-technical teams to quickly access
information hidden in documents, for example, lawyers, business analysts and accountants.[[46]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Dialogue management](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Computer systems intended to converse with a human.[Question answering](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a human-language question, determine its answer.
Typical questions have a specific right answer (such as "What is the capital of Canada?"),
but sometimes open-ended questions are also considered (such as "What is the meaning
of life?").[Text-to-image generation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a description of an image, generate an image
that matches the description.[[47]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Text-to-scene generationGiven a description of a scene,
generate a [3D model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") of the
scene.[[48]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[49]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[Text-to-video](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")Given a description of a video, generate a video that
matches the description.[[50]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[51]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")


## General tendencies and (possible) future directions
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]
Based on long-standing trends in the field, it is possible to extrapolate future
directions of NLP. As of 2020, three trends among the topics of the long-standing
series of CoNLL Shared Tasks can be observed:[[52]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")

- Interest in increasingly abstract, "cognitive" aspects of natural language (1999–2001: shallow parsing, 2002–03: named entity recognition, 2006–09/2017–18: dependency syntax, 2004–05/2008–09 semantic role labelling, 2011–12 coreference, 2015–16: discourse parsing, 2019: semantic parsing).
- Increasing interest in multilinguality, and, potentially, multimodality (English since 1999; Spanish, Dutch since 2002; German since 2003; Bulgarian, Danish, Japanese, Portuguese, Slovenian, Swedish, Turkish since 2006; Basque, Catalan, Chinese, Greek, Hungarian, Italian, Turkish since 2007; Czech since 2009; Arabic since 2012; 2017: 40+ languages; 2018: 60+/100+ languages)
- Elimination of symbolic representations (rule-based over supervised towards weakly supervised methods, representation learning and end-to-end systems)


### Cognition
[[edit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")]
Most higher-level NLP applications involve aspects that emulate intelligent behavior
and apparent comprehension of natural language. More broadly speaking, the technical
operationalization of increasingly advanced aspects of cognitive behavior represents
one of the developmental trajectories of NLP(see trends among CoNLL shared tasks
above).

[Cognition](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") refers to "the mental action or process of acquiring knowledge and understanding through thought, experience, and the
senses."[[53]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
[Cognitive science](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") is the interdisciplinary, scientific study of the mind and its
processes.[[54]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
[Cognitive linguistics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") is an interdisciplinary branch of linguistics, combining knowledge and research from both psychology and
linguistics.[[55]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Especially during the age of [symbolic NLP](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), the area of computational linguistics maintained strong ties with cognitive studies.

As an example, [George Lakoff](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") offers a methodology to build natural language processing (NLP) algorithms through the perspective of cognitive science, along with the findings of cognitive
linguistics,[[56]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
with two defining aspects:

1. Apply the theory of [conceptual metaphor](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), explained by Lakoff as "the understanding of one idea, in terms of another" which provides an idea of the intent of the author.[[57]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") For example, consider the English word *big*. When used in a comparison ("That is a big tree"), the author intends to imply that the tree is *physically large* relative to other trees or the author's experience. When used metaphorically ("Tomorrow is a big day"), the author intends to imply *importance*. The intent behind other usages, like in "She is a big person", will remain somewhat ambiguous to a person and a cognitive NLP algorithm alike without additional information.
2. Assign relative measures of meaning to a word, phrase, sentence or piece of text based on the information presented before and after the piece of text being analyzed, e.g., by means of a [probabilistic context-free grammar](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") (PCFG). The mathematical equation for such algorithms is presented in [US Patent 9269353](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"):[[58]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")

R          M          M          (          t      
o          k          e                      n                          N    
)                =                  P          M     
M          (          t          o          k          e                    
n                          N                                )                ×
1                          2              d         
(                                    ∑  
i                =                −                
d                                            d                                   
(              (              P              M              M   
(              t              o              k              e         
n                                  N                        
)                        ×                          P       
F              (              t              o              k             
e                              n                                  N             
−                  i                                           ,         
t              o              k              e

t              o              k              e                      
n                                  N                  +                  
i                                            )                              )    
i                                                  
)                      {\\displaystyle {RMM(token_{N})}={PMM(token_{N})}\\times
{\\frac {1}{2d}}\\left(\\sum _{i=-d}^{d}{((PMM(token_{N})}\\times
{PF(token_{N-i},token_{N},token_{N+i}))_{i}}\\right)}  
![application/x-wiki](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")*Where***RMM** is the relative measure of meaning**token**
is any block of text, sentence, phrase or word**N** is the number of tokens being
analyzed**PMM** is the probable measure of meaning based on a corpora**d** is the
non-zero location of the token along the sequence of **N** tokens**PF** is the probability
function specific to a language
Ties with cognitive linguistics are part of the historical heritage of NLP, but they
have been less frequently addressed since the statistical turn during the 1990s.
Nevertheless, approaches to develop cognitive models towards technically operationalizable
frameworks have been pursued in the context of various frameworks, e.g., of cognitive
grammar,[[59]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
functional grammar,[[60]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
construction grammar,[[61]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
computational psycholinguistics and cognitive neuroscience (e.g., [ACT-R](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")), however, with limited uptake in mainstream NLP (as measured by presence on major
conferences[[62]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
of the [ACL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")). More recently, ideas of cognitive NLP have been revived as an approach to achieve [explainability](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), e.g., under the notion of "cognitive
AI".[[63]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
Likewise, ideas of cognitive NLP are inherent to neural models [multimodal](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") NLP (although rarely made
explicit)[[64]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
and developments in [artificial intelligence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles"), specifically tools and technologies using [large language model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
approaches[[65]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
and new directions in [artificial general intelligence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles") based on the [free energy principle](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")[[66]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles")
by British neuroscientist and theoretician at University College London [Karl J. Friston](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles").
